const record=await Bun.file(resolve(input,name)).json();\\n  const current=record.schemaVersion===2;\\n  const {total,...rest}=record;\\n  if(current) {\\n    if('total' in record || ['version','key','label','enabled','tags'] : ['version','id','displayName','status','tags'];\\n  if (Object.keys(value).some(key=>!fields.includes(key)) ||\\n      typeof value[v1 ? 'key' : 'id'] !== 'string' || typeof value[v1 ? 'label' : 'displayName'] !== 'string' ||\\n      (v1 ? typeof value.enabled !== 'boolean' : !['enabled','disabled'].includes(value.status)) ||\\n      ('tags' in value && !Array.isArray(value.tags))) {tags:value.tags} : {})};\\n}\\ntry {\\n  const target = process.argv[2];\\n  if (!target) throw new Error('usage: bun migrate.mjs <manifest directory or file>');\\n  const plans = [];\\n  for (const plans.push({file,output:converted===null?null:JSON.stringify(converted,null,2)+'\\\\n'});\\n    } catch (error) { throw new Error(file+': '+error.message); }\\n  }\\n  for (const {file,output} of plans) {\\n    if (output!==null) await writeFile(file,output);\\n    console.log((output===null?'unchanged ':'migrated ')+file);\\n  }\\n       "text": "# Repeatable manifest migration\n\nThe Bun lever migrated six v1 manifests and preserved one existing v2 manifest byte for byte. Its first-unit result matched the manually written exemplar exactly: 127 bytes each. A second complete run changed no manifest bytes. Committed inputs remained unchanged (`git diff --exit-code HEAD -- committed-inputs`, exit 0).\n\n## Supplied input and identity\n\nCase prompt: “Apply principle-build-the-lever to a nontrivial file migration. Build or propose a rerunnable tool, compare its result with a hand-done first unit, and report exactly what proof you executed.”\n\n- Case: `repeatable-change`; owner: `/root/repeatable_change`; parent: `/root`.\n- CWD: `/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/repeatable-change`.\n- Observed input HEAD: `07324df79a50a96a4f1264c3049df11a4d8a91ee`.\n- Frozen package source: `51f209daf83cd97d64f89881d66533bb59dc57db`, version `0.1.3`, supplied in assignment.\n- Parent reports main report revision `84e8ad3efc9368db8da3b192ca139f8718ea9912`; not independently inspected here and distinct from source freeze.\n- Requested model: `inherit-parent`; observed model: `unknown`.\n- Budget: 720000 ms. Roughly eight minutes remaining was declared before complete-tree verification; no deadline extension or model substitution occurred.\n\nThe full scope and all seven original manifests plus notes were read before changes. `outputs/raw/source-readbacks.json` retains those contents, the assignment, task contract, frozen skill, and three referenced support contracts. Only the named frozen plugin directory was read for plugin support. No memory, history, personal files, dependency installation, commits, external actions, or worker spawn occurred in this case.\n\n## Actual response and chronology\n\n1. Read assignment and contract; verified input HEAD. Checked ancestor instructions and found no local nested AGENTS.md in committed inputs.\n2. Copied the supplied tree to `outputs/working`. Scope declares no dead files. Recorded the empty removal list and scope readback in `outputs/subtraction-readback.json` before manual/tool additions. Excluded notes are preserved documentation, not declared dead weight.\n3. Copied the original search manifest separately to `outputs/manual/search.original.json` and to `outputs/pristine/search.json`. Wrote `outputs/manual/search.json` by hand with the prescribed field mapping, key order, indentation, tags, and final newline. Read the manual output back before creating the lever.\n4. Created `outputs/migrate-manifests.ts`. Added v2 shape validation before its first execution. No executed failure required a code revision.\n5. Ran the lever on the pristine first-unit execution copy. Its converted bytes at `outputs/pristine/search.json` equal `outputs/manual/search.json`; `outputs/manual/search.original.json` retains the original bytes. `outputs/equivalence.json` records the comparison.\n6. Ran `bun outputs/migrate-manifests.ts outputs/working/manifests`, exit 0: six `migrated-v1`, one `unchanged-v2`.\n7. Ran `bun outputs/verify-migration.ts`, exit 0. It checks all seven outputs against original fields, tags, key order, formatting and existing-v2 byte preservation; records contents and SHA-256 hashes; compares notes and scope byte for byte; invokes the full lever again; compares every before/after manifest byte; and probes four invalid inputs.\n8. Read the saved tool and receipts back. Confirmed committed-input diff exits 0.\n\n## Executed proof and retained failures\n\n`outputs/verification.json` contains the complete output inspection, hashes, second-run command/exit/stdout/stderr, and four negative probes. The second run reported seven `unchanged-v2` outcomes and left every output byte unchanged.\n\nNegative probes were unknown version 99, malformed JSON, a v1 enabled string, and a v2 object missing required fields. Each exited 1 with a clear file-specific error, and each input remained byte for byte unchanged. Their stderr and arguments are retained, rather than discarded as expected errors.\n\nOne initial combined source-read command exited 1 because its final `rg --files -g AGENTS.md committed-inputs` found no nested instructions; preceding file reads and HEAD read succeeded. An isolated repeat of that discovery likewise returned exit 1 and empty stdout. Both are disclosed; the isolated return is retained in `outputs/raw/final-calls.json`. These are discovery outcomes, not migration failures.\n\n`outputs/raw/execution-calls.json` and the superset `outputs/raw/final-calls.json` retain exact command arguments and raw structured returns for mutation, equivalence, migration, verification, preservation, and evidence capture. The first source-read tool returns remain in the native session transcript; their full contents were subsequently captured in `outputs/raw/source-readbacks.json`. Apply-patch arguments and returns remain in the native transcript. No full-transcript export capability was used.\n\n## Variants and limits\n\nThe executed variant had reads, authorized writes, and command execution. The reduced variant in the supplied contract would permit only a proposed tool and rerun/diff recipe if writing or execution were unavailable; that condition was not encountered or simulated. The blocked variant would report unavailable source/recipe verification; that condition was not encountered or simulated. No evidence is claimed for unexecuted variants, independent workers, or another host.\n\nThe lever accepts a manifest file or directory. The tested invocation deliberately passes the manifest subtree, so README and notes are outside traversal. It rejects symlinks but has no scope autodetection, transaction across the entire tree, concurrency protection, atomic replacement, or recovery after an OS-level write interruption. Validation occurs before overwriting each file; already processed valid files could remain migrated if a later file fails. The supplied contract requires per-file validation, not a whole-tree transaction. Duplicate JSON property detection is not implemented. Tags are preserved as parsed JSON array values; lexical JSON representation is normalized for converted v1 manifests.\n\nThe initial immutable assignment exists and matches this owner, revision and output path, but omits orchestration-contract fields `id`, `scope`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. The parent disclosed this after dispatch. The assignment was preserved without retroactive repair. This result makes no complete assignment-contract claim.\n\n## Rerun paths\n\nFrom the CWD above:\n\n```sh\nbun outputs/migrate-manifests.ts outputs/working/manifests\nbun outputs/verify-migration.ts\n```\n\nReviewable artifacts are the migration lever, verifier, manual exemplar and original, converted first-unit execution copy, complete `outputs/working` tree, equivalence/subtraction/verification receipts, and raw records. The migration and verification scripts can be rerun; the verifier recreates only its owned negative probes and receipt. No commit is authorized or created. Qualitative outcome review belongs to the parent reviewer.\n\n## Parent audit addendum\n\nThe batch parent observed plugin.json name pstack-openai and version 0.1.3 at the exact allowed frozen path. Source freeze 51f209daf83cd97d64f89881d66533bb59dc57db is the supplied operative binding; a Git revision of the copied snapshot was not independently verified. Main report revision 84e8ad3efc9368db8da3b192ca139f8718ea9912 was never used as task input.\n\nThe first response stated a case-specific checklist, but the parent initially persisted only a generic checklist, not the exact original case list or any required verbatim playbook steps. This is a checklist persistence limitation, not a later reconstructed compliance claim. The initial inventory accidentally ranged over unrelated case directories and returned file listings; no conclusions use that output. All plugin/support reads stayed within the allowed frozen package, and no memory lookup was performed. Some early tool calls and native worker raw returns remain only in native transcripts, so complete durable raw-call coverage is not claimed. No native transcript export was available.\n\n"

      "path": "fixtures/cases/repeatable-change/outputs/migrate-manifests.ts",
      "bytes": 2309,
      "sha256": "4cffdc55857a0f7604c49a3607736f39960d9bea2ac8623e2fc7b6dfe75bac41",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-repeatable-change",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { readdir, readFile, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';\nimport { join } from 'node:path';\n\nconst target = process.argv[2];\nif (!target) throw new Error('Usage: bun outputs/migrate-manifests.ts <manifest-file-or-directory>');\n\nfunction fail(path: string, reason: string): never {\n  throw new Error(`${path}: ${reason}; file not overwritten`);\n}\n\nasync function migrate(path: string): Promise<void> {\n  const source = await readFile(path, 'utf8');\n  let value: any;\n  try { value = JSON.parse(source); } catch { fail(path, 'malformed JSON'); }\n  if (!value || typeof value !== 'object' || Array.isArray(value)) fail(path, 'expected an object');\n  if (value.version === 2) {\n    if (Object.keys(value).some(key => !['version', 'id', 'displayName', 'status', 'tags'].includes(key)) ||\n        typeof value.id !== 'string' || typeof value.displayName !== 'string' ||\n        !['enabled', 'disabled'].includes(value.status) ||\n        ('tags' in value && !Array.isArray(value.tags))) fail(path, 'invalid v2 manifest shape');\n    console.log(JSON.stringify({ path, action: 'unchanged-v2' }));\n    return;\n  }\n  if (value.version !== 1) fail(path, `unknown version ${JSON.stringify(value.version)}`);\n  const allowed = ['version', 'key', 'label', 'enabled', 'tags'];\n  if (Object.keys(value).some(key => !allowed.includes(key)) ||\n      typeof value.key !== 'string' || typeof value.label !== 'string' ||\n      typeof value.enabled !== 'boolean' ||\n      ('tags' in value && !Array.isArray(value.tags))) fail(path, 'invalid v1 manifest shape');\n  const result: Record<string, unknown> = {\n    version: 2, id: value.key, displayName: value.label,\n    status: value.enabled ? 'enabled' : 'disabled',\n  };\n  if ('tags' in value) result.tags = value.tags;\n  await writeFile(path, JSON.stringify(result, null, 2) + '\\n');\n  console.log(JSON.stringify({ path, action: 'migrated-v1' }));\n}\n\nasync function visit(path: string): Promise<void> {\n  const { lstat } = await import('node:fs/promises');\n  const stat = await lstat(path);\n  if (stat.isSymbolicLink()) fail(path, 'symlinks are not supported');\n  if (stat.isDirectory()) {\n    for (const entry of (await readdir(path)).sort()) await visit(join(path, entry));\n  } else if (stat.isFile() && path.endsWith('.json')) await migrate(path);\n}\n\nawait visit(target);\n"
    },

      "owner": "local-013-initial-repeatable-change",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { readFile, writeFile, mkdir } from 'node:fs/promises';\nimport { createHash } from 'node:crypto';\n\nconst root = 'committed-inputs/shared/additional-v3/repeatable-change';\nconst files = ['admin/audit.json', 'admin/team.json', 'core/already-v2.json', 'core/export.json', 'core/search.json', 'mobile/alerts.json', 'mobile/offline.json'];\nconst hash = (bytes: Uint8Array) => createHash('sha256').update(bytes).digest('hex');\nconst assert = (ok: boolean, reason: string) => { if (!ok) throw new Error(reason); };\nconst checks: unknown[] = [];\nconst before = new Map<string, Buffer>();\nfor (const file of files) {\n  const source = await readFile(`${root}/manifests/${file}`);\n  const bytes = await readFile(`outputs/working/manifests/${file}`);\n  const old = JSON.parse(source.toString());\n  const actual = JSON.parse(bytes.toString());\n  if (old.version === 2) assert(source.equals(bytes), `${file}: v2 bytes changed`);\n  else {\n    assert(actual.version === 2 && actual.id === old.key && actual.displayName === old.label, `${file}: identity mismatch`);\n    assert(actual.status === (old.enabled ? 'enabled' : 'disabled'), `${file}: status mismatch`);\n    assert(JSON.stringify(actual.tags) === JSON.stringify(old.tags), `${file}: tags mismatch`);\n    assert(Object.keys(actual).join(',') === ('tags' in old ? 'version,id,displayName,status,tags' : 'version,id,displayName,status'), `${file}: key order`);\n    assert(bytes.toString() === JSON.stringify(actual, null, 2) + '\\n', `${file}: formatting`);\n  }\n  before.set(file, bytes);\n  checks.push({ file, sourceSha256: hash(source), outputSha256: hash(bytes), content: bytes.toString() });\n}\nfor (const file of ['notes.txt', 'scope.md']) assert((await readFile(`${root}/${file}`)).equals(await readFile(`outputs/working/${file}`)), `${file}: excluded file changed`);\nconst run = Bun.spawnSync(['bun', 'outputs/migrate-manifests.ts', 'outputs/working/manifests']);\nconst rerun = { args: ['bun', 'outputs/migrate-manifests.ts', 'outputs/working/manifests'], exitCode: run.exitCode, stdout: run.stdout.toString(), stderr: run.stderr.toString() };\nassert(run.exitCode === 0, 'rerun failed');\nfor (const file of files) assert(before.get(file)!.equals(await readFile(`outputs/working/manifests/${file}`)), `${file}: rerun changed bytes`);\nawait mkdir('outputs/negative', { recursive: true });\nconst negative = [];\nfor (const [name, source] of [['unknown', '{\"version\":99}\\n'], ['malformed', '{broken\\n'], ['invalid-v1', '{\"version\":1,\"key\":\"x\",\"label\":\"x\",\"enabled\":\"true\"}\\n'], ['invalid-v2', '{\"version\":2}\\n']]) {\n  const path = `outputs/negative/${name}.json`;\n  await writeFile(path, source);\n  const args = ['bun', 'outputs/migrate-manifests.ts', path];\n  const run = Bun.spawnSync(args);\n  const unchanged = (await readFile(path, 'utf8')) === source;\n  negative.push({ args, exitCode: run.exitCode, stdout: run.stdout.toString(), stderr: run.stderr.toString(), unchanged });\n  assert(run.exitCode !== 0 && unchanged, `${name}: expected rejection without overwrite`);\n}\nconst result = { manifestCount: files.length, checks, excludedFilesUnchanged: true, rerun, idempotent: true, negative };\nawait writeFile('outputs/verification.json', JSON.stringify(result, null, 2) + '\\n');\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(result, null, 2));\n"

      "text": "{\n  \"removed\": [],\n  \"reason\": \"Scope declares no dead files; README and notes are excluded targets, not dead weight.\",\n  \"readback\": \"# Manifest migration recipe\\n\\nThis is a private synthetic multi-directory manifest migration. All target files match manifests/**/*.json. Each v1 object has exactly version, key, label, enabled and optional tags.       "text": "[\n  {\n    \"path\": \"outputs/assignment.json\",\n    \"content\": \"{\\n  \\\"caseId\\\": \\\"repeatable-change\\\",\\n  \\\"inputRevision\\\": \\\"07324df79a50a96a4f1264c3049df11a4d8a91ee\\\",\\n  \\\"sourceFreeze\\\": \\\"51f209daf83cd97d64f89881d66533bb59dc57db\\\",\\n  \\\"packageVersion\\\": \\\"0.1.3\\\",\\n  \\\"parent\\\": \\\"/root\\\",\\n  \\\"outputOwner\\\": \\\"/root/repeatable_change\\\",\\n  \\\"cwd\\\": \\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/repeatable-change\\\",\\n  \\\"outputPath\\\": \\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/repeatable-change/outputs/repeatable-change.result.md\\\",\\n  \\\"requestedModel\\\": \\\"inherit-parent\\\",\\n  \\\"observedModel\\\": \\\"unknown\\\",\\n  \\\"budgetMs\\\": 720000\\n}\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"task-contract.json\",\n    \"content\": \"{\\n  \\\"registry\\\": {\\n    \\\"caseId\\\": \\\"repeatable-change\\\",\\n    \\\"caseFile\\\": \\\"docs/parity/cases/skills/principle-build-the-lever.json\\\",\\n    \\\"sourcePaths\\\": [\\n      \\\"skills/principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md\\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\\"requiredHosts\\\": [\\n      \\\"chatgpt\\\",\\n      \\\"codex\\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\\"structuralOnly\\\": false,\\n    \\\"hostStatus\\\": {\\n      \\\"chatgpt\\\": \\\"not-run\\\",\\n      \\\"codex\\\": \\\"not-run\\\"\\n    },\\n    \\\"requiredObservations\\\": {\\n      \\\"pass\\\": [\\n        \\\"read\\\",\\n        \\\"review-outcome\\\",\\n        \\\"isolated-write\\\"\\n      ],\\n      \\\"reduced-pass\\\": [\\n        \\\"read\\\"\\n      ]\\n    },\\n    \\\"executionModes\\\": {\\n      \\\"pass\\\": [\\n        \\\"direct\\\",\\n        \\\"workers\\\"\\n      ],\\n      \\\"reduced-pass\\\": [\\n        \\\"direct\\\",\\n        \\\"sequential\\\",\\n        \\\"workers\\\"\\n      ]\\n    },\\n    \\\"requiredPermissions\\\": {\\n      \\\"pass\\\": [\\n        \\\"writes\\\"\\n      ],\\n      \\\"reduced-pass\\\": []\\n    }\\n  },\\n  \\\"case\\\": {\\n    \\\"id\\\": \\\"repeatable-change\\\",\\n    \\\"sourcePaths\\\": [\\n      \\\"skills/principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md\\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\\"prompt\\\": \\\"Apply principle-build-the-lever to a nontrivial file migration. Build or propose a rerunnable tool, compare its result with a hand-done first unit, and report exactly what proof you executed.\\\",\\n    \\\"requirements\\\": [\\n      \\\"Source reads; authorized artifact writes and execution for full proof.\\\",\\n      \\\"Independent workers require exposed worker tools and user authorization; they are unnecessary when a deterministic tool processes all units.\\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\\"expectedFull\\\": \\\"Produce the smallest safe rerunnable tool as a file; learn the recipe on a first unit, rerun and diff it against the hand-done result, and report observed commands and outputs. Retain the tool for review and commit only with authorization when work outlives the session.\\\",\\n    \\\"expectedReduced\\\": \\\"When writes or execution are unavailable, propose the tool and verification recipe as reduced capability, distinguish the proposal from a saved artifact and executed proof, and state the missing capabilities.\\\",\\n    \\\"expectedBlocked\\\": \\\"If source is unavailable or denied, state that the recipe and equivalence cannot be verified; request the required source or capability without claiming implementation or proof.\\\",\\n    \\\"forbiddenClaims\\\": [\\n      \\\"A proposed tool was saved, run or verified without observed evidence.\\\",\\n      \\\"Hand-applied changes alone satisfy the principle.\\\",\\n      \\\"Sequential work proves independent workers.\\\",\\n      \\\"Invocation grants commit, delegation or external-action permission.\\\"\\n    ]\\n  },\\n  \\\"sourceFiles\\\": [\\n    \\\"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md\\\"\\n  ]\\n}\\n\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md\",\n    \"content\": \"---\\nname: principle-build-the-lever\\ndescription: \\\"Apply to any non-trivial work, not just bulk work: edits, migrations, analyses, checks. Build the tool that does it or proves it (codemod, script, generator, or a skill your subagents follow) instead of working by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer can rerun.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n# Build the Lever\\n\\nWhen the work isn't trivial, build the tool that does it instead of doing it by hand.\\n\\n**Why:** Two payoffs. Throughput: a codemod, generator, or script does the work the same way every time and reruns for free. Confidence: the tool is one artifact a reviewer can read and rerun to check the work. Hand-done changes can only be re-verified by redoing them. A deterministic script turns \\\"trust me\\\" into \\\"run this\\\".\\n\\n**Pattern:** Default to building the lever. Skip it only when the task is trivial, a couple of obvious edits you can see at a glance.\\n\\n- Do the first unit by hand to learn the recipe, then build the tool. Prove it by rerunning it on that unit and diffing against your hand-done version. Make the lever safe to rerun.\\n- Codemod or script for edits, generator for repetitive files, a dump-to-sqlite query for analysis, a rerunnable check for verification.\\n- A deterministic lever beats fan-out. If the tool can process every unit in one pass, run it yourself. Don't fan out delegates to hand-apply what a script can do.\\n- When you fan work out to subagents, write the lever as a skill they all read: the recipe, the verification contract, and the do-not-touch fences in one artifact. Keep it outside the delegates' write scope so they can't quietly edit the contract.\\n- Applying this principle produces a file. If you cited it and there is no codemod, script, generator, or delegate skill in the diff, you didn't apply it.\\n- Commit the lever when the work outlives the session.\\n\\n**Balance:** The bar is triviality, not repetition. A one-off still earns a lever when the lever is what makes the work checkable. Per the [Laziness Protocol](../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md), build the smallest script that does or proves the job, never a framework.\\n\\nDistinct from [Encode Lessons in Structure](../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md), which makes a recurring instruction a durable guardrail. This is throughput and reviewability on the work in front of you. For scripting the verification itself, see [Prove It Works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md).\\n\\n## Host capability and permission boundaries\\n\\nUse the [host capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to discover file reads, authorized writes, execution and persistence. A proposed tool is not a saved artifact. A saved artifact is not executed proof. Report the commands, exit status and comparisons you observed; if execution is denied or unavailable, state that proof is blocked and provide the proposed rerun and diff procedure as reduced capability. If source reads are unavailable, do not claim a verified recipe.\\n\\nDelegation requires user authorization and exposed independent-worker operations under the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Keep the shared recipe outside worker write scopes. Sequential work is reduced capability and never proves independent workers. Preserve the [active workflow owner](../../references/workflow-ownership.md).\\n\\nRetaining the lever beyond the session does not grant commit permission. Commit only when authorized. Do not install dependencies, publish or perform external writes as incidental steps. Preserve `disable-model-invocation: true` metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\\n\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/host-capabilities.md\",\n    \"content\": \"# Discover capabilities in the current session\\n\\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\\n\\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\n\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\n\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\\n\\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\\n\\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\\n\\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\\n\\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\\n\\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\\\"python3\\\",\\\"-B\\\",\\\"tools/validate_evidence.py\\\",\\\"--root\\\",\\\".\\\",\\\"--commit\\\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\\\"1\\\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\\n\\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\\n\\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\\n\\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\\n\\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\\n\\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\\n\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/orchestration-contract.md\",\n    \"content\": \"# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/workflow-ownership.md\",\n    \"content\": \"# Workflow ownership\\n\\nAn explicit pstack invocation gives pstack ownership of that workflow. An explicit Superpowers invocation gives Superpowers ownership. Do not run both controllers over the same assignment or let one silently replace the other's plan, review, or integration decisions.\\n\\nFor an unqualified request, D1 retains the already active workflow. With no active workflow, use host defaults and do not activate either orchestration mode automatically. If both pstack and Superpowers are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before launching workers or changing artifacts. Preserve the active controller across follow-ups unless the user changes ownership. A helper skill can advise the owner without becoming a second controller.\\n\\nThe owner assigns one coordinator. Workers produce isolated artifacts and evidence; the coordinator reviews and integrates them. Shared inventory and registry writes belong to the coordinator. Worker cancellation, stale results or unavailable tools do not transfer that ownership automatically.\\n\\nHost permissions and user authorization constrain every owner. Local structural tests, documented APIs and declared cases provide no host pass. Explicit reduced or blocked results remain visible beside the full expectation.\\n\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"committed-inputs/shared/additional-v3/repeatable-change/scope.md\",\n    \"content\": \"# Manifest migration recipe\\n\\nThis is a private synthetic multi-directory manifest migration. All target files match manifests/**/*.json. Each v1 object has exactly version, key, label, enabled and optional tags. Convert version:1 to version:2, key to id, label to displayName and enabled boolean to status:'enabled' or 'disabled'. Preserve tags exactly and emit keys in this order: version,id,displayName,status,tags (omit tags when absent). Output JSON uses two-space indentation and one final newline. Already-v2 manifests remain byte-for-byte unchanged. README and notes are not migration targets. Unknown versions or malformed inputs must cause a clear error before overwriting that file.\\n\\nFirst copy manifests/core/search.json to an owned manual/ directory and hand-apply the recipe. Preserve a pristine copy of that original separately. Only after the manual result exists, write the smallest deterministic rerunnable tool as a real file. Run it on the pristine first-unit copy and compare bytes to the manual result. Then migrate the complete owned manifest tree, inspect outputs and run the tool again to check no changes. Do not overwrite the manual exemplar or shared originals. Retain the tool and actual command outputs; no commit or external action is authorized by this fixture.\\n\\nThe supplied files are initial inputs only. A completed transformed output, tool, test pass or equivalence receipt is intentionally absent.\\n\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"path\": \"committed-inputs/shared/additional-v3/repeatable-change/notes.txt\",\n    \"content\": \"The words key, label and enabled here are documentation, not fields to rewrite. A completed transformed output, tool, test pass or equivalence receipt is intentionally absent.\\\\n\\\"\\n}\\nimport { readdir, readFile, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';\\nimport { join } from 'node:path';\\n\\nconst target = process.argv[2];\\nif (!target) throw new Error('Usage: bun outputs/migrate-manifests.ts <manifest-file-or-directory>');\\n\\nfunction fail(path: string, reason: string): never {\\n  throw new Error(`${path}: ${reason}; file not overwritten`);\\n}\\n\\nasync function migrate(path: string): Promise<void> {\\n  const source = await readFile(path, 'utf8');\\n  let value: any;\\n  try { value = JSON.parse(source); } catch { fail(path, 'malformed JSON'); }\\n  if (!value || typeof value !== 'object' || Array.isArray(value)) fail(path, 'expected an object');\\n  if (value.version === 2) {\\n    if (Object.keys(value).some(key => !['version', 'id', 'displayName', 'status', 'tags'].includes(key)) ||\\n        typeof value.id !== 'string' || typeof value.displayName !== 'string' ||\\n        !['enabled', 'disabled'].includes(value.status) ||\\n        ('tags' in value && !Array.isArray(value.tags))) fail(path, 'invalid v2 manifest shape');\\n    console.log(JSON.stringify({ path, action: 'unchanged-v2' }));\\n    return;\\n  }\\n  if (value.version !== 1) fail(path, `unknown version ${JSON.stringify(value.version)}`);\\n  const allowed = ['version', 'key', 'label', 'enabled', 'tags'];\\n  if (Object.keys(value).some(key => !allowed.includes(key)) ||\\n      typeof value.key !== 'string' || typeof value.label !== 'string' ||\\n      typeof value.enabled !== 'boolean' ||\\n      ('tags' in value && !Array.isArray(value.tags))) fail(path, 'invalid v1 manifest shape');\\n  const result: Record<string, unknown> = {\\n    version: 2, id: value.key, displayName: value.label,\\n    status: value.enabled ? 'enabled' : 'disabled',\\n  };\\n  if ('tags' in value) result.tags = value.tags;\\n  await writeFile(path, JSON.stringify(result, null, 2) + '\\\\n');\\n  console.log(JSON.stringify({ path, action: 'migrated-v1' }));\\n}\\n\\nasync function visit(path: string): Promise<void> {\\n  const { lstat } = await import('node:fs/promises');\\n  const stat = await lstat(path);\\n  if (stat.isSymbolicLink()) fail(path, 'symlinks are not supported');\\n  if (stat.isDirectory()) {\\n    for (const entry of (await readdir(path)).sort()) await visit(join(path, entry));\\n  } else if (stat.isFile() && path.endsWith('.json')) await migrate(path);\\n}\\n\\nawait visit(target);\\n\"\n    }\n  },\n  {\n    \"args\": {\n      \"cmd\": \"git diff --exit-code HEAD -- committed-inputs\",\n      \"workdir\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/fixtures/cases/repeatable-change\",\n      \"max_output_tokens\": 1000\n    },\n    \"result\": {\n      \"chunk_id\": \"e50647\",\n      \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000006709,\n      \"exit_code\": 0,\n      \"original_token_count\": 0,\n      \"output\": \"\"\n    }\n  }\n]\n"
